# Illinois — Election Directives, Guidance & Procedure Manuals

Dataset: State Election Rules and Guidance. Official guidance content for Illinois, compiled for the VB Election Desk knowledge base. Every section below cites the official source it was captured from and the publisher's own currency statement; sources were verified as official publications before inclusion.
Sections: 4

## Illinois SBE — "Guidance for Election Authorities" (statutorily required guidance on voting, early voting, vote by mail, curbside voting), per election cycle

- **Publisher:** Illinois State Board of Elections
- **Category:** guidance

As required by statute, this document is intended to give election authorities guidance on voting and elections for the upcoming 2027 Consolidated Election Cycle." 2026 edition first page: "Rev. 10/2025". ### Illinois SBE — 2026 Guidance for Election Authorities (2026 election cycle)

*Source: https://www.elections.il.gov/agencyforms/1%20GUIDE%20FOR%20ELECTION%20AUTHORITIES/2026%20Guidance%20for%20Election%20Authorities.pdf. Fetched: 2026-09-10 (first seen 2026-09-10). Publisher's currency statement: 2027 edition first page: "Rev. 07/2026 ... STATE BOARD OF ELECTIONS | STATE OF ILLINOIS / 2027 Guidance for Election Authorities ... As required by statute, this document is intended to give election authorities guidance on voting and elections for the upcoming 2027 Consolidated Election Cycle." 2026 edition first page: "Rev. 10/2025". Directory listing mtimes: 7/29/2026 for the 2027 file, 10/8/2025 for the 2026 file. Source verified official on 2026-09-10. Capture note: Captured from the Board's static publication directory, which lists one Guidance edition per election cycle; the edition's own 'Rev.' date is on its first page.*

Rev. 10/2025 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
STATE BOARD OF ELECTIONS | STATE OF ILLINOIS 
2026 Guidance for Election Authorities 
Voting | Early Voting | Vote by Mail | Curbside Voting 
 
As required by statute, this document is intended to give election authorities guidance on voting and elections 
for the upcoming 2026 General Primary and General Election. Much of this guidance is drawn from the text of 
the Illinois Election Code and it is cited as necessary within this document. Please note that this is not a 
comprehensive overview of each of the responsibilities and duties of election authorities, but a shorthand 
reference for many critical tasks that election authorities handle during election years. For further guidance, 
please reference the 2026 Guide for Election Authorities, 2026 Election Calendar, and other publications on the 
SBE website. 
Requirements for Participation in Elections 
All individuals intending to vote in an Illinois election must be registered.  
 
Regular registration is open year-round except during the 27-day period just prior to an election, 16 days prior to 
an election for online registration, and during the 2-day period after each election (1 day after in Chicago). 
Grace period registration is an extension of the regular registration deadline from the 27th day prior to an 
election through Election Day. Grace period registration is only available in person at sites authorized by each 
election authority (e.g. not through the SBE’s online voter registration portal).  
(10 ILCS 5/4 et seq.,5/5 et seq, and 5/6 et seq.) 
 
If an individual registered by mail, they must vote in person the first time (either at the polling place or by early 
voting), unless they submitted with their mail-in registration form their driver’s license number or state ID 
number, the last four digits of their social security number, or one of the forms of ID listed below.  
• A current and valid photo identification 
• Utility bill 
• Bank statement 
• Government check 
• Paycheck 
• Lease or contract for residence 
• Student ID & mail addressed to voter’s residence 
• Government document 
 
(10 ILCS 5/4-105, 5-105, 6-105) 
The above-listed forms of ID must be current, show the voter’s name and address, and must be presented to 
the election officials before being permitted to vote. If they do not present a required form of identification either 
at the time of submitting their mail-in registration or at the time of voting, they may vote a Provisional Ballot.  
(10 ILCS 5/18A-5) Voters voting under the provisions of UOCAVA and the Voting Accessibility for the Elderly 
and Handicapped Act do not have to vote in person for the first time after having registered by mail.  
(52 U.S.C. 21082) 
  

 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
 
 Jail Voting  
Regardless of an individual’s criminal record, anyone who is not serving a sentence of confinement in a penal 
institution is eligible to vote in Illinois. (10 ILCS 5/3-5)  
 
In counties with a population under 3,000,000 the election authority must collaborate with the primary county jail 
where eligible voters are confined or detained to facilitate an opportunity for voting by mail by eligible voters in 
the election authority’s jurisdiction who are confined or detained in the county jail. Only a resident of the county 
who is in custody at the county jail and who has not been convicted of the offense for which the resident is in 
custody is eligible to vote. Additionally, sheriffs may establish temporary branch polling places in the county jails 
of these counties. (10 ILCS 5/19-2.3,19A-20(e)(2))  
 
Cook County (a county with a population over 3,000,000) must establish a temporary branch polling place in the 
county jail. Only a resident of the county who is in custody at the county jail and who has not been convicted of 
the offense for which the resident is in custody is eligible to vote. (10 ILCS 5/19A-20(e)(1))  
 
Additionally, each election authority must collaborate with the county jail within their jurisdiction to facilitate voter 
registration for voters who are eligible to vote in that county who are confined or detained in county jail. The 
county jail must provide a voter registration application to any person in custody at the jail who requests an 
application and is eligible to vote. (55 ILCS 5/3-15003.3)  
Provisional Voting 
Any person who claims to be a registered voter is entitled to cast a provisional ballot under the following 
circumstances: 
 
• The voter’s name does not appear on the official list of eligible voters for the precinct in which the person 
seeks to vote and the person has refused an opportunity to register; 
• The voter’s voting status has been challenged and a majority of the judges uphold the challenge;  
• A court order extends the time for closing the polls, and the voter votes during this extended time period;  
• The voter did not provide identification when registering by mail and does not provide ID on Election Day; 
• The voter's name appears on the list of voters who voted during the early voting period, but the voter 
claims not to have voted during the early voting period; 
• The voter received a vote by mail ballot, but failed to return it to the election authority (or election judges); 
or 
• The voter attempted to register to vote on Election Day, but failed to provide the necessary documentation. 
(10 ILCS 5/18A-5(a)) 
 
The provisional voter may submit additional supporting information to the election authority to show why their 
ballot should be counted. The additional information must be received by the election authority within seven 
days following the election. The election authority has 14 days after the election to determine voter eligibility, 
and thus, if the ballot should be counted. The provisional ballots will then be counted or left sealed in the 
provisional ballot envelopes. The election authority will add the number of approved provisional ballots to the 
Election Day results. The provisional voter may find out whether their ballot was counted and, if it was not 
counted, the reason it was not counted. If the ballot is not counted, the provisional voter affidavit will be used as 
a registration application and the voter will be sent a voter’s registration ID card. (10 ILCS 5/18A-15,18A-20) 
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
Curbside Voting 
General Guidelines  
Election authorities must establish curbside voting for voters with a temporary or permanent disability who 
cannot physically enter the polling place to cast a ballot during early voting or on Election Day. Election 
authorities have the option to extend curbside voting to all voters.  Curbside voting provides the voter with the 
option to remain in their vehicle while marking and completing their ballot. (10 ILCS 5/17-13(b), 17-13.5) 
 
Required: For Voters Who are Temporarily or Permanently Disabled and Cannot Enter the Polling Place  
 
• The ballot delivered to the voter may not be taken beyond 50 feet of the entrance to the building in 
which the polling place is located. (10 ILCS 5/17-13(b)) 
• Requests for curbside assistance should be made no later than the close of business at the election 
authority’s office on the day before the election. Election authorities must notify election judges of the 
appropriate precinct polling places of requests for curbside voting. (10 ILCS 5/17-13(b)) 
• No assistance may be given to the voter in marking their ballot unless the voter requests assistance and 
completes the appropriate affidavit. (10 ILCS 5/17-13(d)) 
 
Optional: Establishment of Curbside Voting for All Voters 
 
• Election authorities have the option of establishing curbside voting for individuals to cast a ballot during 
early voting or on Election Day. (10 ILCS 5/17-13.5) 
• This would not require the voter to make a request a day prior to Election Day. (10 ILCS 5/17-13.5) 
• An election authority’s curbside voting program would designate at least 2 election judges from opposite 
parties per vehicle and the individual will be given the opportunity to mark the ballot  without leaving their 
car. (10 ILCS 5/17-13.5) 
Early Voting 
General Guidelines 
An early voting ballot may be applied for only by personal appearance and must be voted on the premises of the 
election authority, except as otherwise provided, and returned to the election authority.  (10 ILCS 5/19A-5) 
 
The period for early voting by personal appearance begins the 40
th day preceding an election and extends 
through the end of the day before the election. For the 2026 General Primary, this date is Thursday, February 5, 
2026. For the 2026 General Election, the date is Thursday, September 24, 2026. (10 ILCS 5/19A-15(a)) 
 
Establishment of Permanent Polling Places for Early Voting 
An election authority may establish permanent polling places for early voting at locations throughout the election 
authority’s jurisdiction including, but not limited to: 
 
• A municipal clerk’s office 
• A township clerk’s office 
• A road district clerk’s office 
• A county or local public agency office 
 
These units of government who make their buildings available as temporary or permanent polling places must 
ensure that the space made available for voting is accessible to all voters. Any person entitled to vote early by 
personal appearance may do so at any polling place established for early voting. (10 ILCS 5/19A-21) 
  
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
 
In counties with a population over 250,000:  
 
• Applies to each general primary and general election 
• At least one permanent polling place in each of the 3 largest municipalities within jurisdiction  
• If any of these municipalities has a population greater than 80,000, there should be at least 2 permanent 
polling places within the municipality 
• Population figures are determined by the federal census 
(10 ILCS 5/19A-10(c)) 
 
Municipal Board of Election Commissioners with a population over 100,000:  
 
• Applies to each general primary and general election 
• At least two permanent polling places 
• Population figures are determined by the federal census 
(10 ILCS 5/19A-10(d)) 
 
In counties with a population over 100,000 but under 250,000:  
 
• Applies to each general primary and general election 
• At least one permanent polling place whose location may be election authority’s main office or 
elsewhere 
• The election authority may designate additional sites for early voting 
• Population figures are determined by the federal census 
(10 ILCS 5/19A-10(e)) 
 
No permanent polling place shall be located within 1.5 miles from another permanent polling place required by 
Section 19A of the Election Code, unless such permanent polling place is within a municipality with a population 
of 500,000 or more. (10 ILCS 5/19A-10(f)) 
 
Permanent Polling Place Hours 
Permanent polling places for early voting must be open beginning the 15
th day before an election during the 
hours of 8:30 a.m. – 4:30 p.m. or 9:00 a.m. – 5:00 p.m. on weekdays. For the 2026 General Primary, this date is 
Monday, March 2, 2026. For the 2026 General Election, this date is Monday, October 19, 2026. 
 
From the 8
th day preceding the election through the day before the election, permanent polling places must be 
open:  
 
• Weekdays: 8:30 a.m. – 7:00 p.m. or 9:00 a.m. – 7:00 p.m. 
• Saturdays and holidays:  9:00 a.m. – 12:00 p.m. 
• Sundays: 10:00 a.m. – 4:00 p.m.  
For the 2026 General Primary this date is Monday, March 9, 2026. For the 2026 General Election the date is 
Monday, October 26, 2026. 
 
Final Weekend: 
 
• Must be open a total of 14 hours, including the mandatory hours listed above . 
 
Holidays: 
 
• Jurisdictions over 100k must remain open for a total of at least 8 hours on any holiday . 
• This applies to the entire 40-day period for early voting. 
(10 ILCS 5/19A-10) 
 
An election authority may close an early voting polling place if the building in which the polling place is located 
has been closed by the State or unit of local government in response to a severe weather emergency or other 
force majeure. The election authority shall notify the SBE of any closure and shall make reasonable efforts to 
provide notice to the public of an alternative location. (10 ILCS 5/19A-15(c)) 
 
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
Temporary Branch Polling Places 
In addition to permanent polling places for early voting, the election authority may establish temporary branch 
polling places for early voting. Voting at a temporary branch polling place may be conducted on one or more 
days and during any hours within the period for early voting that are determined by the election authority. The 
schedules for conducting voting do not need to be uniform among the temporary branch polling places.  
(10 ILCS 5/19A-20) 
 
Public University Voting 
In addition to the early voting conducted at locations otherwise required by law, appropriate election authorities 
may conduct early voting, grace period registration, and grace period voting at the student union on the campus 
of a public university within the election authority's jurisdiction.  
 
The public universities included in required university voting are: 
 
• University of Illinois 
• Illinois State University 
• Chicago State University 
• Governors State University 
• Southern Illinois University 
• Northern Illinois University 
• Eastern Illinois University 
• Western Illinois University 
• Northeastern Illinois University 
 
If an election authority has voting equipment that can accommodate a ballot in every form required in the 
election authority’s jurisdiction, then the election authority shall extend voting at these universities’ student 
unions to any registered voter in the election authority’s jurisdiction. If it cannot, then the election authority may 
limit university voting to voters in precincts where the public university is located and precincts bordering the 
university. 
 
This voting must be conducted from the 6th day before Election Day until and including the 4th day before 
Election Day from 10:00 a.m. to 5 p.m. For the 2026 General Primary, this date is Wednesday, March 11, 2026. 
For the 2026 General Election, this date is Wednesday, October 28, 2026.  
(10 ILCS 5/1-12) 
 
Pollwatchers for Early Voting 
Each candidate, political party, or organization of citizens may appoint one pollwatcher for each location where 
early voting is conducted. These pollwatchers shall qualify and be appointed in the same manner as those who 
serve on election day. Pollwatchers shall be permitted to challenge for cause the qualifications of voters, 
observe all proceedings, and view all reasonably requested records relating to the conduct of the early voting, 
provided the secrecy of the ballot is not impinged and that they do not interfere with the orderly conduct of 
voting. (10 ILCS 5/19A-60) 
 
Schedule for Early Voting 
The election authority shall publish a schedule for early voting during the week before the period for early voting, 
and at least once each week during the period for early voting, in a newspaper of general circulation in the 
election authority’s jurisdiction. The schedule must state: 
 
• The location of each permanent and temporary polling place for early voting;  
• Precincts served by each location; and, 
• Dates and hours that early voting will be conducted at each location. 
 
The schedule must also be: 
 
• Posted continuously beginning the 10
th day before early voting begins and ending on the last day of the 
early voting period; 
• Posted at any office or other location that is to be used as a polling place for early voting ; 
• Available to the public through copies made and distributed by the election authority without charge; 
and, 
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
• Made available on the election authority’s website. 
 
No additional permanent polling places for early voting may be established after the schedule is published, but 
additional temporary locations may be established after the schedule is published, provided that the location is 
open to all eligible voters. (10 ILCS 5/19A-25(e) and (f)) The location, dates, and hours shall be reported to the 
SBE and posted on the election authority’s website. At least 10 days before the period for early voting begins, 
each election authority shall provide the SBE with a list of all early voting sites and the hours each site will be 
open. (10 ILCS 5/19A-25) 
 
Reporting Requirements 
The election authority shall maintain a list of all voters who have applied for and/or voted a vote by mail, early, or 
grace period ballot. Within one day of posting this list, the election authority shall transmit by electronic means 
pursuant to a process  established by the SBE the voter’s name, street address, email address, precinct, ward, 
township, and district numbers, as the case may be, to the SBE, which shall maintain those names and that 
information in an electronic format on its website, arranged by county and accessible to State and local political 
committees. Additionally, a list of voters who have applied for a vote by mail ballot shall be posted within one 
business day of receipt in a conspicuous, open, and public place at the entrance of the office of the  election 
authority, and so that the list may be viewed without needing permission.  
(10 ILCS 5/4-50, 5-50, 6-100, 19-4, 19-7, 19A-35)   
Vote by Mail 
Completed Ballots Returned by Mail, In-Person, and at Collection Sites 
Vote by mail ballots can be returned by mail, in-person at the election authority’s office, or at collection sites. If 
collection sites are used, the election authority shall collect all ballots submitted each day at the close of 
business and process them as required. Upon collection, the election authority shall note the day on which the 
ballot was collected. All collection sites shall be secured by locks that may be opened only by election 
authority personnel. Ballots submitted to these collection sites after business hours shall be dated as delivered 
the next day. (10 ILCS 5/19-6)  
 
Election authorities shall permit voters to return ballots until the close of the polls on Election Day . These ballots 
shall be endorsed by the receiving election authority with the day and hour of receipt, and may be processed by 
the election authority beginning on the day it is received in the central ballot counting location. (10 ILCS 5/19- 8) 
 
After Election Day, vote by mail ballots will be considered returned timely if they were postmarked on or before 
Election Day. Absent a postmark, the date on the certification will be used to determine timeliness. Ballots 
received after Election Day without a postmark and date of certification shall not be counted. The election 
authority may also use an intelligent mail barcode tracking system to verify that a ballot was mailed no later than 
Election Day. If a ballot is mailed to the election authority with insufficient or no postage, the election authority 
shall accept the ballot regardless of postage due. (10 ILCS 5/19-6,19-8) 
 
Accessible Vote by Mail 
Illinois voters with a print disability are eligible, upon application,  to receive their ballot by electronic transmission 
and utilize assistive technology to complete their ballot.   
 
The State Board of Elections provides a certified remote accessible vote by mail system through which a vote by 
mail ballot can be delivered by electronic transmission to voters with print disabilities and through which they are 
able to mark and verify their ballots using assistive technology. Election authorities shall permit voters with a print 
disability to receive and mark their vote by mail ballots either through the system provided by the State Board of 
Elections or through a certified remote accessible vote by mail system provided by the election authority.  
 
If a vote by mail ballot application from a voter with a print disability arrives after the jurisdiction begins sending 
ballots and instructions to voters, the election authority shall electronically transmit the ballot, instructions, and 
balloting materials to the voter within two business days after receipt of the application. Applications, both single-
election and permanent, for Accessible Vote by Mail for voters with print disabilities must be distinct from standard 
Vote by Mail applications and contain language pursuant to section 19-3 of the Election Code. (10 ILCS 5/19-2.6)  
 
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
Processing of Vote by Mail Ballots 
After the election authority receives a vote by mail ballot, the ballot should be processed as soon as possible. As 
long as the results are not generated until the polls close on the day of the election, ballots can still be 
processed. It is recommended that election authorities process vote by mail ballots as soon as they are received 
to give a voter whose ballot was rejected a sufficient amount of time to submit any evidence necessary to prove 
their ballot should be counted. (10 ILCS 5/19-8) 
 
Review of Vote by Mail Ballots 
Within two days after a vote by mail ballot is received (or before the end of the period for counting provisional 
ballots), the election judge or official shall compare the voter’s signature on the certification envelope  of the vote 
by mail ballot with the signature of the voter on file in the office of the election authority. (10 ILCS 5/19- 8)(g)) 
 
A vote by mail ballot may be rejected by the election judge or official if: 
 
• The signature on the certification envelope does not match the voter’s signature on file;  
• The voter is not qualified to cast a vote by mail ballot; 
• The ballot envelope was delivered opened, or has been opened and resealed;  
• The voter has already cast a ballot;  
• The voter voted in person on Election Day; or 
• On any other basis provided by law. 
 
If the election judge or official determines that any of these reasons apply, the judge or official shall , without 
opening the certification envelope, mark across the face of the certification envelope the word “Rejected” and 
shall not cast or count the ballot. (10 ILCS 5/19-8(g)) 
 
Notice of Rejected Ballot and Reinstatement 
If a vote by mail ballot is rejected, the election authority shall notify the voter within two days after the rejection 
or within one day if the rejection occurs after Election Day, and in all cases before the close of the period for 
counting provisional ballots. This is why the election authority should process the ballot as soon as it is received 
to make sure all rejections are processed in a timely manner. (10 ILCS 5/19-8(g-5)) 
 
The voter shall be notified through mail or email, or both, and the notice shall inform the voter of the reason(s) 
the ballot was rejected and shall state that the voter may appear before the election authority, on or before the 
14
th day after the election, to show cause as to why the ballot should not be rejected. The voter may present 
evidence to the election authority supporting their contention that the ballot should be counted. All vote by mail 
ballots determined to be valid shall be added to the vote totals for the precincts for which they were cast in the 
order in which the ballots were opened. (10 ILCS 5/19-8(g-10))  
 
Invalid or Uncounted Ballots 
Invalid or uncounted vote by mail ballots and special write-in vote by mail blank ballots received by the election 
authority after the closing of the polls on an Election Day shall be endorsed by the election authority receiving 
them with the day and hour of receipt and shall be safely kept unopened by the election authority for the period 
of time required for the preservation of ballots used at the election (22 months), and shall then, without being 
opened, be destroyed in like manner as the used ballots of that election. (52 U.S.C. 20701; 10 ILCS 5/19-8(e)) 
 
Pollwatchers for Vote by Mail 
Where certain vote by mail voters’ ballots are counted in the office of the election authority, each political party, 
candidate, and qualified civic organization shall be entitled to have present one pollwatcher for each panel of 
election judges therein assigned. Such pollwatchers shall be permitted to observe the election judges making 
the signature comparison between that which is on the ballot envelope and that which is on the permanent voter 
registration record card taken from the master file. (10 ILCS 5/19-10)  
 
Damaged and Overvoted Ballot Sheets 
If necessary, remake all damaged or bent ballot sheets that cannot be counted by the tabulating equipment.   
(10 ILCS 5/24B-14) The duplicate ballot is marked exactly the same as the original ballot. Remake all ballots 
that contain an overvote due to an invalid write-in vote. In this case, remake the duplicate ballot sheet exactly as 
the original sheet, but omit the invalid write-in. Remake all sheets that have been folded anywhere other than at 
the designated pre-fold area. (10 ILCS 5/24B-10(a)(2), 24B-14) 
  
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
 
Remaking Procedures: 
At least two judges, one from each major political party, shall remake the duplicate ballot sheet in the following 
manner (10 ILCS 5/24B-10.1): 
 
1) Use an official ballot sheet of the same ballot style. 
2) Mark the duplicate ballot with a specialized pen, if necessary. 
3) One judge reads the candidates/referenda choices to be marked on the duplicate sheet while the other 
judge marks the ballot sheet according to the instructions that are described on the polling place 
Instruction Card. 
4) Check the duplicate ballot sheet by exchanging duties, i.e., the judge who marked the ballot sheet reads 
the names of the candidates/referenda choices marked. 
5) Consecutively number each pair of duplicate ballot sheets. The first damaged ballot sheet is marked 
“Damaged Ballot No. 1" and the corresponding duplicate sheet is marked “Duplicate Damaged Ballot 
No. 1” The next pair is marked “Damaged Ballot No. 2,” and the corresponding duplicate sheet is 
marked “Duplicate Damaged Ballot No. 2."  Continue the process until all duplicated b allot sheets have 
been marked. Likewise, the first pair of overvoted ballot sheets are marked “Overvoted Ballot No. 1,” 
and “Duplicated Overvoted Ballot No. 1.” Continue the process until all duplicated overvoted ballots are 
marked and numbered consecutively. Use a pen with red ink to identify the ballot sheets. 
6) Make sure to initial both the original ballot sheet and the duplicated ballot sheet  
7) Place the original ballot sheet(s) in the envelope for duplicated ballots and place the duplicated ballot 
sheet in the ballot return box with the other valid ballot sheets. Make certain that the Precinct Tabulation 
Optical Scan Technology equipment readout agrees with the number of voters who have returned vote 
by mail ballots.  
Vote Centers 
Election authorities shall establish at least one vote center location to be located at an office of the election 
authority or in the largest municipality within its jurisdiction where all voters in its jurisdiction are allowed to vote 
on election day during polling place hours, regardless of the precinct in which they are registered. If the 
population of the jurisdiction exceeds 500,000, two locations shall be established. (10 ILCS 5/11- 8) 
 
The election authority must provide the following information to the State Board of Elections:  
• Location of Election Day vote centers 
• Hours of operation 
• Health and safety requirements (if applicable) 
• Deadline for the 2026 General Primary: Thursday, February 5, 2026 
• Deadline for the 2026 General Election: Thursday, September 24, 2026

### Illinois SBE — 2027 Guidance for Election Authorities (2027 election cycle)

Rev. 07/2026 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
STATE BOARD OF ELECTIONS | STATE OF ILLINOIS 
2027 Guidance for Election Authorities 
Voting | Early Voting | Vote by Mail | Curbside Voting 
 
As required by statute, this document is intended to give election authorities guidance on voting and elections 
for the upcoming 2027 Consolidated Election Cycle. Much of this guidance is drawn from the text of the Illinois 
Election Code and it is cited as necessary within this document. Please note that this is not a comprehensive 
overview of each of the responsibilities and duties of election authorities, but a shorthand reference for many 
critical tasks that election authorities handle during election years. For further guidance, please reference the 
2027 Election Calendar and other publications on the SBE website. 
Requirements for Participation in Elections 
All individuals intending to vote in an Illinois election must be registered.  
 
Regular registration is open year-round except during the 27-day period just prior to an election, 16 days prior to 
an election for online registration, and during the 2-day period after each election (1 day after in Chicago). 
Grace period registration is an extension of the regular registration deadline from the 27th day prior to an 
election through Election Day. Grace period registration is only available in person at sites authorized by each 
election authority (e.g. not through the SBE’s online voter registration portal). For the consolidated primary, 
grace period registration begins January 27, 2027. For the consolidated election, grace period registration 
begins March 10, 2027.  
(10 ILCS 5/4 et seq.,5/5 et seq, and 5/6 et seq.) 
 
If an individual registered by mail, they must vote in person the first time (either at the polling place or by early 
voting), unless they submitted with their mail-in registration form their driver’s license number or state ID 
number, the last four digits of their social security number, or one of the forms of ID listed below.  
• A current and valid photo identification 
• Utility bill 
• Bank statement 
• Government check 
• Paycheck 
• Lease or contract for residence 
• Student ID & mail addressed to voter’s residence 
• Government document 
The above-listed forms of ID must be current, show the voter’s name and address and must be presented to the 
election officials before being permitted to vote. If they do not present a required form of identification either at 
the time of submitting their mail-in registration or at the time of voting, they may vote a Provisional Ballot. (10 
ILCS 5/18A-5) Voters voting under the provisions of UOCAVA and the Voting Accessibility for the Elderly and 
Handicapped Act do not have to vote in person for the first time after having registered by mail. ( 52 U.S.C. § 
21082) 
 
 

 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
 Jail Voting  
Regardless of an individual’s criminal record, anyone who is not serving a sentence of confinement in a penal 
institution is eligible to vote in Illinois. (10 ILCS 5/3-5)  
 
In counties with a population under 3,000,000 the election authority must collaborate with the primary county jail 
where eligible voters are confined or detained to facilitate an opportunity for voting by mail by eligible voters the 
election authority’s jurisdiction who are confined or detained in the county jail. Additionally, sheriffs may 
establish temporary branch polling places in the county jails of these counties. (10 ILCS 5/19-2.3,19A-20(e)(2))  
 
Cook County (a population over 3,000,000) must establish a temporary branch polling place in the county jail. 
Only a resident of the county who is in custody at the county jail and who has not been convicted of the offense 
and which the resident is in custody is eligible to vote. (10 ILCS 5/19A-20(e)(1))  
 
Additionally, each election authority must collaborate with the county jail within their jurisdiction to facilitate voter 
registration for voters who are eligible to vote in that county who are confined or detained in county jail. The 
county jail must provide a voter registration application to any person in custody at the jail who requests an 
application and is eligible to vote. (55 ILCS 5/3-15003.3)  
Provisional Voting 
Any person who claims to be a registered voter is entitled to cast a provisional ballot under the following 
circumstances: 
 
• The voter’s name does not appear on the official list of eligible voters for the precinct in which the person 
seeks to vote and the person has refused an opportunity to register; 
• The voter’s voting status has been challenged and a majority of the election judges uphold the challenge; 
• A court order extends the time for closing the polls, and the voter votes during this extended time period;  
• The voter did not provide identification when registering by mail and does not provide ID on Election Day; 
• The voter's name appears on the list of voters who voted during the early voting period, but the voter 
claims not to have voted during the early voting period; 
• The voter received a vote by mail ballot, but failed to return it to the election authority (or election judges); 
or 
• The voter attempted to register to vote on Election Day, but failed to provide the necessary documentation. 
(10 ILCS 5/18A-5(a)) 
 
The provisional voter may submit additional supporting information to the election authority to show why their 
ballot should be counted. The additional information must be received by the election authority within 7 days 
following the election. The election authority has 14 days after the election to determine voter eligibility, and 
thus, if the ballot should be counted. The provisional ballots will then be counted or left sealed in the provisional 
ballot envelopes. The election authority will add the number of approved provisional ballots to the Election Day 
results. The provisional voter may find out whether their ballot was counted and, if it was not counted, the 
reason it was not counted. If the ballot is not counted, the provisional voter affidavit will be used as a registration 
application and the voter will be sent a voter’s registration ID card. (10 ILCS 5/18A-15,18A-20) 
 
 
 
 
 
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
Curbside Voting 
General Guidelines  
Election Authorities must establish curbside voting for voters with a temporary or permanent disability who 
cannot physically enter the polling place to cast a ballot during early voting or on Election Day. Election 
authorities have the option to extend curbside voting to all voters.  Curbside voting provides the voter with the 
option to remain in their vehicle while marking and completing their ballot. (10 ILCS 5/17-13(b), 17-13.5) 
 
Required: For Voters Who have a Temporary or Permanent Disability and Cannot Enter the Polling Place 
 
• The ballot delivered to the voter may not be taken beyond 50 feet of the entrance to the building in 
which the polling place is located. (10 ILCS 5/17-13(b)) 
• Requests for curbside assistance should be made no later than the close of business at the election 
authority’s office on the day before the election. Election authorities must notify election judges of the 
appropriate precinct polling places of requests for curbside voting. (10 ILCS 5/17-13(b)) 
• No assistance may be given to the voter in marking their ballot unless the voter requests assistance and 
completes the appropriate affidavit. (10 ILCS 5/17-13(d)) 
 
Optional: Establishment of Curbside Voting for All Voters 
 
• Election Authorities have the option of establishing curbside voting for individuals to cast a ballot during 
early voting or on Election Day. 
• This would not require the voter to make a request a day prior to Election Day. 
• An election authority’s curbside voting program would designate at least 2 election judges from opposite 
parties per vehicle and the individual will be given the opportunity to mark the ballot  without leaving their 
car.  
(10 ILCS 5/17-13.5) 
Early Voting 
General Guidelines 
An early voting ballot may be applied for only by personal appearance and must be voted on the premises of the 
election authority, except as otherwise provided, and returned to the election authority.  (10 ILCS 5/19A-5) 
 
The period for early voting by personal appearance begins the 40
th day preceding an election and extends 
through the end of the day before the election. For the consolidated primary, in-person early voting begins 
January 14, 2027. For the consolidated election, in-person early voting begins February 25, 2027.  
(10 ILCS 5/19A-15(a)) 
 
Establishment of Permanent Polling Places for Early Voting 
An election authority may establish permanent polling places for early voting at locations throughout the election 
authority’s jurisdiction including, but not limited to: 
 
• A municipal clerk’s office 
• A township clerk’s office 
• A road district clerk’s office 
• A county or local public agency office 
 
These units of government who make their buildings available as temporary or permanent polling places must 
ensure that the space made available for voting is accessible to all voters. Any person entitled to vote early by 
personal appearance may do so at any polling place established for early voting. (10 ILCS 5/19A-21) 
 
No permanent polling place shall be located within 1.5 miles from another permanent polling place required by 
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
Section 19A of the Election Code, unless such permanent polling place is within a municipality with a population 
of 500,000 or more. (10 ILCS 5/19A-10(f)) 
 
Permanent Polling Place Hours 
Permanent polling places for early voting must be open beginning the 15th day before an election during the 
hours of 8:30 a.m. – 4:30 p.m. or 9:00 a.m. – 5:00 p.m. on weekdays. For the consolidated primary, this date is 
February 8, 2027, and for the consolidated election, this date is March 22, 2027. 
 
From the 8th day preceding the election (February 15, 2027, for the consolidated primary and March 29, 2027, 
for the consolidated election) through the day before the election, permanent polling places must be open:  
 
• Weekdays: 8:30 a.m. – 7:00 p.m. or 9:00 a.m. – 7:00 p.m. 
• Saturdays and holidays:  9:00 a.m. – 12:00 p.m. 
• Sundays: 10:00 a.m. – 4:00 p.m.  
 
Final Weekend: 
 
• Must be open a total of 14 hours, including the mandatory hours listed above . 
 
Holidays: 
 
• Jurisdictions over 100k must remain open for a total of at least 8 hours on any holiday . 
• This applies to the entire 40-day period for early voting. 
(10 ILCS 5/19A-10) 
 
An election authority may close an early voting polling place if the building in which the polling place is located 
has been closed by the State or unit of local government in response to a severe weather emergency or other 
force majeure. The election authority shall notify the SBE of any closure and shall make reasonable efforts to 
provide notice to the public of an alternative location. (10 ILCS 5/19A-15(c)) 
 
Temporary Branch Polling Places 
In addition to permanent polling places for early voting, the election authority may establish temporary branch 
polling places for early voting. Voting at a temporary branch polling place may be conducted on one or more 
days and during any hours within the period for early voting that are determined by the election authority. The 
schedules for conducting voting do not need to be uniform among the temporary branch polling places.  
(10 ILCS 5/19A-20) 
 
Public University Voting 
In addition to the early voting conducted at locations otherwise required by law, appropriate election authorities 
may conduct early voting, grace period registration, and grace period voting at the student union on the campus 
of a public university within the election authority's jurisdiction.  
 
The public universities included in required university voting are: 
 
• University of Illinois 
• Illinois State University 
• Chicago State University 
• Governors State University 
• Southern Illinois University 
• Northern Illinois University 
• Eastern Illinois University 
• Western Illinois University 
• Northeastern Illinois University 
 
If an election authority has voting equipment that can accommodate a ballot in every form required in the 
election authority’s jurisdiction, then the election authority shall extend voting at these universities’ student 
unions to any registered voter in the election authority’s jurisdiction.
 If it cannot, then the election authority may 
limit university voting to voters in precincts where the public university is located and precincts bordering the 
university. 
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
 
This voting must be conducted from the 6th day before Election Day (February 17, 2027, for the consolidated 
primary. March 31, 2027, for the consolidated election) until and including the 4th day before Election Day 
(February 19, 2027, for the consolidated primary. April 2, 2027, for the consolidated election.) from 10:00 a.m. to 
5 p.m.  
(10 ILCS 5/1-12) 
 
Pollwatchers for Early Voting 
Each candidate, political party, or organization of citizens may appoint one pollwatcher for each location where 
early voting is conducted.  These pollwatchers shall qualify and be appointed in the same manner as those who 
serve on election day. Pollwatchers must be residents of the State and possess valid pollwatcher credentials. 
Pollwatchers shall be permitted to challenge for cause the qualifications of voters, observe all proceedings, and 
view all reasonably requested records relating to the conduct of the early voting, provided the secrecy of the 
ballot is not impinged and that they do not interfere with the orderly conduct of voting. (10 ILCS 5/19A-60) 
 
Schedule for Early Voting 
The election authority shall publish a schedule for early voting during the week before the period for early voting, 
and at least once each week during the period for early voting, in a newspaper of general circulation in the 
election authority’s jurisdiction. The schedule must state: 
 
• The location of each permanent and temporary polling place for early voting;  
• Precincts served by each location; and, 
• Dates and hours that early voting will be conducted at each location. 
 
The schedule must also be: 
 
• Posted continuously beginning the 10th day before early voting begins (January 4, 2027, for the 
consolidated primary. February 16, 2027, for the consolidated election.) and ending on the last day of 
the early voting period (February 22, 2027, for the consolidated primary. April 5, 2027, for the 
consolidated election.); 
• Posted at any office or other location that is to be used as a polling place for early voting ; 
• Available to the public through copies made and distributed by the election authority without charge; 
and, 
• Made available on the election authority’s website. 
 
No additional permanent polling places for early voting may be established after the schedule is published, but 
additional temporary locations may be established after the schedule is published, provided that the location is 
open to all eligible voters. (10 ILCS 5/19A-25(e) and (f)) The location, dates, and hours shall be reported to the 
SBE and posted on the election authority’s website. At least 10 days before the period for early voting begins, 
each election authority shall provide the SBE with a list of all early voting sites and  the hours each site will be 
open. (10 ILCS 5/19A-25) 
 
Reporting Requirements 
The election authority shall maintain a list of all voters who have applied for and/or voted a vote by mail, early, or 
grace period ballot. Within one day of posting this list, the election authority shall transmit by electronic means 
pursuant to a process  established by the SBE the voter’s name, street address, email address, precinct, ward, 
township, and district numbers, as the case may be, to the SBE, which shall maintain those names and that 
information in an electronic format on its website, arranged by county and accessible to State and local political 
committees. Additionally, a list of voters who have applied for a vote by mail ballot shall be posted within one 
business day of receipt in a conspicuous, open, and public place at the entrance of the office of the  election 
authority, and so that the list may be viewed without needing permission.  
(10 ILCS 5/4-50, 5-50, 6-100, 19-4, 19-7, 19A-35)   
 
 
 
 
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
Vote by Mail 
Completed Ballots Returned by Mail, In-Person, and at Collection Sites/ Ballot Drop Box Locations 
Vote by mail ballots can be returned by mail, in-person at the election authority’s office, or at drop box locations. 
If drop boxes are used, the election authority, their employees, election judges (not including high school 
students serving as election judges), election officials, or other designated officials  shall collect all ballots 
submitted each day at the close of business and process them as required. Upon collection, the election 
authority shall note the day on which the ballot was collected. All drop boxes shall be secured by locks that may 
be opened only by election authority personnel. Ballots submitted to these drop boxes after business hours 
shall be dated as delivered the next day. (10 ILCS 5/19-6)  
 
Election authorities shall permit voters to return ballots until the close of the polls on Election Day . These ballots 
shall be endorsed by the receiving election authority with the day and hour of receipt, and may be processed by 
the election authority beginning on the day it is received in the central ballot counting location. (10 ILCS 5/19- 8) 
 
After Election Day, vote by mail ballots will be considered returned timely if they were postmarked on or before 
Election Day. Absent a postmark, the date on the certification will be used to determine timeliness. Ballots 
received after Election Day without a postmark and date of certification shall not be counted. The election 
authority may also use an intelligent mail barcode tracking system to verify that a ballot was mailed no later than 
Election Day. If a ballot is mailed to the election authority with insufficient or no postage, the election authority 
shall accept the ballot regardless of postage due. (10 ILCS 5/19-6,19-8) 
 
Accessible Vote by Mail 
Illinois voters with a print disability are eligible, upon application,  to receive their ballot by electronic transmission 
and utilize assistive technology to complete their ballot.   
 
The State Board of Elections provides a certified remote accessible vote by mail system through which a vote by 
mail ballot can be delivered by electronic transmission to voters with print disabilities and through which they are 
able to mark and verify their ballots using assistive technology. Election authorities shall permit voters with a print 
disability to receive and mark their vote by mail ballots either through the system provided by the State Board of 
Elections or through a certified remote accessible vote by mail system provided by the election authority.  
 
If a vote by mail ballot application from a voter with a print disability arrives after the jurisdiction begins sending 
ballots and instructions to voters, the election authority shall electronically transmit the ballot, instructions, and 
balloting materials to the voter within two business days after receipt of the application. Applications, both single-
election and permanent, for Accessible Vote by Mail for voters with print disabilities must be distinct from standard 
Vote by Mail applications and contain language pursuant to section 19-3 of the Election Code. (10 ILCS 5/19-2.6)  
 
Processing of Vote by Mail Ballots 
After the election authority receives a vote by mail ballot, the ballot should be processed as soon as possible. As 
long as the results are not generated until the polls close on the day of the election, ballots can still be 
processed. It is recommended that election authorities process vote by mail ballots as soon as they are received 
to give a voter whose ballot was rejected a sufficient amount of time to submit any evidence necessary to prove 
their ballot should be counted. (10 ILCS 5/19-8) 
 
Review of Vote by Mail Ballots 
Within two days after a vote by mail ballot is received (or before the end of the period for counting provisional 
ballots), the election judge or official shall compare the voter’s signature on the certification envelope  of the vote 
by mail ballot with the signature of the voter on file in the office of the election authority. (10 ILCS 5/19- 8(g)) 
 
A vote by mail ballot may be rejected by the election judge or official if: 
 
• The signature on the certification envelope does not match the voter’s signature on file;  
• The voter is not qualified to cast a vote by mail ballot; 
• The ballot envelope was delivered opened, or has been opened and resealed;  
• The voter has already cast a ballot;  
• The voter voted in person on Election Day; or 
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
• On any other basis provided by law. 
If the election judge or official determines that any of these reasons apply, the judge or official shall , without 
opening the certification envelope, mark across the face of the certification envelope the word “Rejected” and 
shall not cast or count the ballot. (10 ILCS 5/19-8(g)) 
 
Notice of Rejected Ballot and Reinstatement 
If a vote by mail ballot is rejected, the election authority shall notify the voter within two days after the rejection 
or within one day if the rejection occurs after Election Day, and in all cases before the close of the period for 
counting provisional ballots. This is why the election authority should process the ballot as soon as it is received 
to make sure all rejections are processed in a timely manner. (10 ILCS 5/19-8(g-5)) 
 
The voter shall be notified through mail or email, or both, and the notice shall inform the voter of the reason(s) 
the ballot was rejected and shall state that the voter may appear before the election authority, on or before the 
14th day after the election (March 9, 2027, for the consolidated primary. April 20, 2027, for the consolidated 
election), to show cause as to why the ballot should not be rejected. The voter may present evidence to the 
election authority supporting their contention that the ballot should be counted. All vote by mail ballots 
determined to be valid shall be added to the vote totals for the precincts for which they were cast in the order in 
which the ballots were opened. (10 ILCS 5/19-8(g-10))  
 
Invalid or Uncounted Ballots 
Invalid or uncounted vote by mail ballots and special write-in vote by mail blank ballots received by the election 
authority after the closing of the polls on an Election Day shall be endorsed by the election authority receiving 
them with the day and hour of receipt and shall be safely kept unopened by the election authority for the period 
of time required for the preservation of ballots used at the election (22 months), and shall then, without being 
opened, be destroyed in like manner as the used ballots of that election. (52 U.S.C. § 20701; 10 ILCS 5/19-8(e)) 
 
Pollwatchers for Vote by Mail 
Where certain vote by mail voters’ ballots are counted in the office of the election authority, each political party, 
candidate, and qualified civic organization shall be entitled to have present one pollwatcher for each panel of 
election judges therein assigned. Such pollwatchers shall be permitted to observe the election judges making 
the signature comparison between that which is on the ballot envelope and that which is on the permanent voter 
registration record card taken from the master file. (10 ILCS 5/19-10)  
 
Damaged and Overvoted Ballot Sheets 
If necessary, remake all damaged or bent ballot sheets that cannot be counted by the tabulating equipment.  (10 
ILCS 5/24B-14) The duplicate ballot is marked exactly the same as the original ballot. Remake all ballots that 
contain an overvote due to an invalid write-in vote. In this case, remake the duplicate ballot sheet exactly as the 
original sheet, but omit the invalid write-in. Remake all sheets that have been folded anywhere other than at the 
designated pre-fold area. (10 ILCS 5/24B-10(a)(2),24B-14) 
 
Remaking Procedures: 
At least two judges, one from each major political party, shall remake the duplicate ballot sheet in the following 
manner (10 ILCS 5/24B-10.1): 
 
1) Use an official ballot sheet of the same ballot style. 
2) Mark the duplicate ballot with a specialized pen, if necessary. 
3) One judge reads the candidates/referenda choices to be marked on the duplicate sheet while the other 
judge marks the ballot sheet according to the instructions that are described on the polling place 
Instruction Card. 
4) Check the duplicate ballot sheet by exchanging duties, i.e., the judge who marked the ballot sheet reads 
the names of the candidates/referenda choices marked. 
5) Consecutively number each pair of duplicate ballot sheets. The first damaged ballot sheet is marked 
“Damaged Ballot No. 1" and the corresponding duplicate sheet is marked “Duplicate Damaged Ballot 
No. 1” The next pair is marked “Damaged Ballot No. 2,” and the corresponding duplicate sheet is 
marked “Duplicate Damaged Ballot No. 2."  Continue the process until all duplicated b allot sheets have 
been marked. Likewise, the first pair of overvoted ballot sheets are marked “Overvoted Ballot No. 1,” 
and “Duplicated Overvoted Ballot No. 1.” Continue the process until all duplicated overvoted ballots are 
marked and numbered consecutively. Use a pen with red ink to identify the ballot sheets. 
6) Make sure to initial both the original ballot sheet and the duplicated ballot sheet.  
 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
7) Place the original ballot sheet(s) in the envelope for duplicated ballots and place the duplicated ballot 
sheet in the ballot return box with the other valid ballot sheets. Make certain that the Precinct Tabulation 
Optical Scan Technology equipment readout agrees with the number of voters who have returned vote 
by mail ballots.  
Vote Centers 
Election authorities shall establish at least one location to be located at an office of the election authority or in 
the largest municipality within its jurisdiction where all voters in its jurisdiction are allowed to vote on election day 
during polling place hours, regardless of the precinct in which they are registered. If the population of the 
jurisdiction exceeds 500,000, two locations shall be established. (10 ILCS 5/11-8) 
 
The election authority must provide the following information to the State Board of Elections  by the 40
th day 
preceding an election: 
• Location of Election Day vote centers 
• Hours of operation 
• Health and safety requirements (if applicable) 
Deadline to provide the required information to the SBE: 
• consolidated primary: January 14, 2027 
• consolidated election: February 25, 2027

2 
 
Introduction 
This Guide for election authorities for the March 17, 2026, General Primary and the November 3, 
2026, General Election contains election administration procedures required by the Help America 
Vote Act of 2002 (“HAVA”) and includes information on Illinois State election law. It is designed to 
assist election officials in implementing Federal and State procedures and to provide statewide 
guidance for the general primary and general elections. 
 
This Guide focuses on Election Day procedures, including pre- and post-election procedures, 
accessibility issues, the Military and Overseas Voter Empowerment (“MOVE”) Act, National Change 
of Address (“NCOA”), and the Electronic Registration Information Center (“ERIC”). It is not intended 
to be an exhaustive recitation of Federal and State law, but rather its purpose is to provide a helpful 
tool for election authorities to use and reference. This Guide may be amended to include new 
legislation and court decisions. Please visit the Illinois State Board of Elections’ IVRS website under 
Support/Documents for any updates. 
 
In addition, it should be noted that the material in this Guide represents the best 
understanding and interpretation of election law by the Illinois State Board of Elections 
(“SBE”). It should not be considered a definitive legal opinion by the agency, and it should 
be understood that the agency cannot and does not provide advisory opinions. In the case 
of any unresolved questions requiring legal interpretation, election authorities should 
consult with their own state’s attorney. 
Other Publications 
The State Board of Elections also produces additional guides that can be helpful when preparing to 
run for office. The Election and Campaign Finance Calendar lists more specific dates and deadlines 
to follow and the Candidate’s Guide has specific information pertaining to each office up for election 
in 2026. You can find both of these publications on the State Board of Elections’ 
website under the 
“Running for Office” menu and under the “Publications” tab. 
Contact Information 
 
 
 
 
Chicago Office 
69 West Washington Street 
Pedway LL-08 
Chicago, IL 60602 
Phone: (312) 814-6440 
Fax: (312) 814-6485 
webmaster@elections.il.gov 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
Springfield Office 
2329 South MacArthur Boulevard 
Springfield, Illinois 62704 
Phone: (217) 782-4141 
Fax: (217) 782-5959 
webmaster@elections.il.gov 
www.elections.il.gov 
 
3 
 
Pre-Election Day Procedures 
VOTER REGISTRATION 
Voter registration in Illinois is governed by all of the following: (1) the Illinois Election Code; (2) the 
National Voter Registration Act and its implementing rules and regulations; and (3) the Help 
America Vote Act.  
 
Age Requirements – Registering to Vote and Circulating Petitions 
A person who is 17 years old on the date of a primary election, and is otherwise qualified to vote, is 
qualified to vote at that primary election, so long as that person will be 18 years old on the date of 
the immediately following general election. They are also deemed competent to execute and attest 
to any voter registration forms. 
 
A person who is qualified to vote may preregister to vote on or after their 16
th birthday using the 
online voter registration website, ova.elections.il.gov, or by registering with an automatic voter 
registration agency. The registration application is then held by the State Board of Elections until 
that person reaches the required age to vote. The State Board of Elections will then transmit the 
registration application to the applicable election authority. They are also deemed competent to 
execute and attest to any voter registration forms.  
 
A person who is 17 years old and will be 18 years old on the date of the immediately following 
general election and is otherwise qualified to vote, is eligible to circulate a nominating petition or a 
petition proposing a public question. 
(10 ILCS 5/3-6) 
 
Name Changes 
Any registered voter who changes their name, by marriage or otherwise, is required to register 
anew and authorize the cancellation of their previous registration. If the voter still resides in the 
same precinct the elector may, if otherwise qualified, vote upon making an affidavit at the polling 
place attesting that the voter is the same person who is registered to vote under their former name. 
The affidavit shall be treated by the election authority as authorization to cancel the registration 
under the former name and the election authority shall register the person under their current 
name. Forms for this purpose shall be provided by the election authority and shall, in all cases, be 
similar to the form furnished by the election authority for County and State elections.  
(10 ILCS 5/4-16, 5-23, 6-54)   
 
Voter Registration Forms 
The State Board of Elections is required to provide an internet registration form in both English 
(SBE Form R-19) and Spanish (SBE Form R-19A) that can be mailed or delivered by the applicant 
either to the applicant’s election authority or the SBE. Election authorities may also design and print 
their own registration forms as long as the requirements of 10 ILCS 5/1A-16, 4-8, 5-7 and 6-35 are 
met. All registration forms, whether created by an election authority, internet-based, or the duplicate 
or triplicate forms provided for in the Election Code, must include a space for the voter to include 
either an Illinois Driver’s License ID number or the last four digits of the applicant’s social security 
number, and an option for the voter to enter an email address. The forms must also have a space 
for a person without a driver’s license or a social security number to provide a Secretary of State ID 
number. If the voter has none of the prescribed ID numbers, they must so indicate and the State 
must assign this voter a unique identifier.  
4 
 
 
The SBE, election authorities, and designated agencies must provide any member of the public 
“with any reasonable number of forms that they may request.” In addition, election authorities must 
provide any additional registration forms requested by a deputy registrar, regardless of how many 
unaccounted forms they may have in their possession. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16(e), 4-6.2, 5-16.2, 6-50.2) 
 
Registering by Mail 
Under the provisions of HAVA, a person who registers in Illinois using a mail registration form must 
answer the following questions:  
 
1) “Are you a citizen of the United States of America?”, and  
2) “Will you be 18 years of age on or before Election Day?”  
 
If the applicant answers “NO” to either question, the form indicates that the applicant should not 
complete the form. If the applicant does not answer either question, the election authority must 
notify the applicant that the form is incomplete and provide them an opportunity to complete the 
form. (52 USC 21083(b)(4)(A) and (B)) 
For more required contents on a voter registration form, see 10 ILCS 5/1A-16(d)(1-15).  
 
ID Requirements for First Time Voters  
People who register to vote for the first time in a jurisdiction by mail must provide identification 
when submitting their mail-in registration form. Acceptable forms of identification are: 
1. The person’s driver’s license number, State identification card number, or if the person does 
not have either of those;  
2. Verification by the last four digits of the person’s social security number; 
3. A copy of a current and valid photo identification, or a copy of a current utility bill, bank 
statement, paycheck, government check; 
4. Other Federal, State, or local government document that shows the person’s name and 
address; or, 
5. A photo identification issued by a college or university accompanied by either a copy of the 
applicant’s contract or lease for a residence or any postmarked mail delivered to the 
applicant at their current residence address.  
 
If the registrant does not provide ID when applying for registration by mail, they shall be notified by 
the election authority that their registration has not been fully completed and that they remain 
ineligible to vote by mail or in person until such proof is presented. The voter is required to provide 
ID the first time they vote. If a voter who is required to provide ID when voting for the first time 
cannot provide ID on Election Day, the voter may cast a provisional ballot. The voter must then 
provide ID to the election authority within 7 calendar days after the election or the provisional ballot 
will not be counted. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16(a), 4-105, 5-105, 6-105, 18A-5(a)(d)) 
 
Note: This does not apply to individuals who have successfully registered to vote with the online 
voter registration program, except:  
 
1) If the election authority can match the driver’s license number or the last 4 digits of the 
individual’s social security number with an existing State identification record bearing the 
same number, name,  and date of birth, the voter will not be required to provide 
identification when registering or voting (including voting by mail or early voting);  
 (52 U.S.C. 21083(b)(3)(B)(ii)) 
5 
 
2) Citizens who are entitled to vote under the Uniformed and Overseas Citizens Absentee 
Voting Act (52 U.S.C. 20301, et seq.) (i.e. members of the military service, spouses, and/or 
their dependents who accompany them on active service, and citizens temporarily residing 
overseas) do not have to provide copies of identification when registering to vote by mail. 
(10 ILCS 5/20-1, 20-2, 20-2.1) 
 
Processing Registration Forms 
Election authorities are required to accept registration forms that are printed on regular stock or 
photocopier paper. Forms which must be accepted are: 
 
1) Mail registrations postmarked on or before the close of regular registration; 
2) Not postmarked but delivered no later than 5 days after the close of regular registration; 
3) Registrations submitted in person on or before the close of regular registration; 
4) Grace period registration voters during the period after regular registration through Election 
Day; or, 
5) Submitted in person on or before the close of regular registration by a person who submits 
forms on behalf of others. 
(10 ILCS 5/1A-16(b), 4-50, 5-50, 6-100) 
 
Forms that are delivered to the SBE must be date-stamped and forwarded to the appropriate 
election authority within 2 business days of receipt. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16(b), (e)) 
 
All applications submitted on a website maintained by the SBE shall be deemed timely filed if they 
are submitted no later than 11:59 p.m. on the 16th day prior to an election. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(l)) 
See the “Online Voter Registration” section below for more details.  
 
Signature Requirements 
The making and signing of any form may be by a signature written in ink or in digitized form. This 
includes an application to register, a certificate authorizing cancellation of a registration, a 
certificate authorizing a transfer of registration, an application to vote, a provisional ballot, or an 
affidavit. This does not include a nominating or candidate petition, or referendum petition.  
(10 ILCS 5/1-13) 
 
Online Voter Registration  
The SBE has established and maintains a system for online voter registration that permits a person 
to apply to register to vote, or to update their existing voter registration.  
 
In accordance with technical specifications provided by the SBE, each election authority shall 
maintain a voter registration system capable of receiving and processing voter registration 
application information and including electronic signature images, from the SBE’s online voter 
registration system, and shall employ security measures to ensure the accuracy and integrity of 
voter registration applications submitted electronically.  
 
The SBE may receive voter registration information provided by applicants using the SBE’s 
website, cross-reference that information with data or information contained in the Secretary of 
State’s database in order to match the information submitted by applicants, and receive from the 
Secretary of State the applicant’s digitized signature upon a successful match of that applicant’s 
information with that contained in the Secretary of State’s database.  
 
6 
 
Certain State agencies that maintain a website must provide a link to online voter applications as 
outlined in Section 1A-16.5 of the Election Code. Notwithstanding any other provision of law, a 
person who is qualified to register to vote and who has an authentic Illinois driver’s license or State 
identification card issued by the Secretary of State may submit an application to register to vote 
electronically on a website maintained by the SBE. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5, 4-33, 5-43, 6-79) 
 
Paperless Online Voter Application (POVA) Requirements 
An online voter registration application shall contain all of the information that is required for a 
paper application as provided in Section 1A-16 of the Election Code, except that the applicant shall 
be required to provide:  
 
1) Their full Illinois driver’s license or State identification card number;   
2) The last 4 digits of their social security number; and,  
3) The date the Illinois driver’s license or State identification card was issued. 
(10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(e))   
 
For an applicant’s registration or change in registration to be accepted, the applicant shall mark the 
box associated with the following statement included as part of the online voter registration 
application: “By clicking on the box below, I swear or affirm all of the following:  
 
1) I am the person whose name and identifying information is provided on this form, and I 
desire to register to vote in the State of Illinois. 
2) All the information I have provided on this form is true and correct as of the date I am 
submitting this form.  
3) I authorize the Secretary of State to transmit to the State Board of Elections my signature 
that is on file with the Secretary of State and understand that such signature will be used by 
my election authority on this online voter registration application for admission as an elector 
as if I had signed this form personally.” (10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(f)) 
 
Immediately upon receiving a completed online voter registration application, the online voter 
registration system shall send, by email, a confirmation notice that the application has been 
received. Within 48 hours of receiving such an application, the online voter registration system shall 
send by email, a notice informing the applicant of whether the following information has been 
matched with the Secretary of State database:  
 
1) The applicant has an authentic Illinois driver’s license or State identification card issued by 
the Secretary of State and that the driver’s license or State identification number provided by 
the applicant matches the driver’s license or State identification card number for that person 
on file with the Secretary of State;   
2) The date of issuance of the Illinois driver’s license or State identification card listed on the 
application matches the date of issuance of that card for that person on file with the 
Secretary of State;  
3) The date of birth provided by the applicant matches the date of birth for that person on file 
with the Secretary of State; and,  
4) The last 4 digits of the applicant’s social security number match the last four digits for that 
person on file with the Secretary of State. 
(10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(g))  
 
7 
 
If the information provided by the applicant matches the information on the Secretary of State’s 
databases for any driver’s license and State identification card holder and is matched as provided 
in the above section, the online voter registration system shall:  
 
1) Retrieve from the Secretary of State’s database files an electronic copy of the applicant’s 
signature from their Illinois driver’s license or State identification card and such signature 
shall be deemed to be the applicant’s signature on their online voter application; 
2) Within 2 days of receiving the application, forward to the county clerk or board of election 
commissioners having jurisdiction over the applicant’s voter registration: 
(i) the application, along with the applicant’s relevant data that can be directly loaded 
into the jurisdiction’s voter registration system, and  
(ii) a copy of the applicant’s electronic signature and a certification from the SBE that the 
applicant’s driver’s license or State identification card number, driver’s license or 
State identification card date of issuance, and date of birth and social security 
information have been successfully matched.   
(10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(h)) 
 
Processing the POVA 
Upon receipt of the online voter registration application, the county clerk or board of election 
commissioners having jurisdiction over the applicant’s voter registration shall promptly search its 
voter registration database to determine whether the applicant is already registered to vote at the 
address on the application and whether the new registration would create a duplicate registration.  
 
If the applicant is already registered to vote at the address on the application, the county clerk or 
board of election commissioners shall send the applicant by first class mail, and email if the 
applicant has provided an email address on the original voter registration form, a disposition notice 
as otherwise required by law informing the applicant that they are already registered to vote at such 
address.  
 
If the applicant is not already registered to vote at the address on the application and the applicant 
is otherwise eligible to register to vote, the county clerk or board of election commissioners shall:  
 
1) Enter the name and address of the applicant on the list of registered voters in the 
jurisdiction; and,   
2) Send by mail and email, if the applicant has provided an email address on the voter 
registration form, a disposition notice to the applicant as otherwise provided by law setting 
forth the applicant’s name and address as it appears on the application, and state that the 
person is registered to vote. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(i)) 
 
Any new registration or change of address submitted electronically shall become effective as of the 
date it is received by the county clerk or board of election commissioners having jurisdiction over 
said registration. Disposition notices prescribed in Section 16.5 of the Election Code shall be sent 
within 5 business days of receipt of the online application or change of address by the county clerk 
or board of election commissioners. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(k)) 
 
All applications submitted on a website maintained by the SBE shall be deemed timely filed if they 
are submitted by 11:59 p.m. on the 16th day prior to an election. After the regular registration period 
for an upcoming election has ended and until the 2nd day following such election, the web page 
8 
 
containing the online voter registration form on the SBE’s website will inform users of the procedure 
for grace period registration and voting. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(l)) 
 
 
Duplicate Paperless Online Voter Applications 
An electronic signature of the person submitting a duplicate registration application or a change of 
address form that is retrieved and imported from the Secretary of State’s driver’s license or State 
identification card database may, in the discretion of the county clerk or board of election 
commissioners, be substituted for and replace any existing signature for that individual in the voter 
registration database of the county clerk or board of election commissioners. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(j))  
 
The SBE shall maintain a list of the name, street address, email address, and likely precinct, ward, 
township, and district numbers, as the case may be, of people who apply to vote online through the 
voter registration system. Those names and that information shall be stored in an electronic format 
on its website arranged by county and accessible to State and local political committees. Each 
state department that maintains an Internet website must include a hypertext link to the homepage 
website maintained and operated pursuant to Section 1A-16.5 of the Election Code.  
(10 ILCS 5/1A-16.5(m), (p)) 
 
Email Notification to Voters 
The voter registration form includes a space giving individuals the option to include their email 
address. If an individual includes their email address on the form, election authorities may send 
Registration Cards, Notice of Erasure from Registration Records, Notice of Duplicate Registration, 
Notice of Registration Cancellation, Notice of Transfer of Registration, and Confirmation of Address 
to the person via email as well as via postal mail.   
 
If an election authority is required by law to send an election related notice to an individual, that 
election authority may send that notice solely by email if the individual provides a current email 
address to the election authority and authorizes the election authority to send notices by email.  
All voter information received by the election authorities shall be transmitted to and then stored in 
an electronic format on the SBE website, arranged by county and accessible to State and local 
political committees.  
(10 ILCS 5/1A-16(d)(15), 1A-16.5, 4-8, 4-10, 4-12, 4-15, 5-7, 5-9, 5-15, 5-21, 6-29, 6-35, 6-40, 6-
57) 
 
Grace Period Registration and Voting 
Grace period registration and voting authorizes each election authority to establish procedures for 
the registration of voters and for change of addresses for all voters, including eligible residents at 
certain elder care facilities. Although the regular registration period closes 28 days prior to the 
election, grace period registration extends that deadline from the 27
th day prior to an election 
through Election Day. Grace period registration is only available in person at sites designated by 
the election authority (and in some jurisdictions, at polling places on Election Day). This extension 
is only available through the election authority, not through the deputy registrars.  
(10 ILCS 5/4-50, 5-50, 6-100) 
 
Government Agency Voter Registration 
The SBE has established and maintains a portal for government agency registration that permits 
individuals to electronically apply to register to vote or update their voter registration when they 
conduct business, either online or in person, with designated State agencies. The SBE’s portal 
9 
 
interfaces with the online voter registration system and is capable of receiving and processing voter 
registration application information, including electronic signatures, from the designated agencies. 
(10 ILCS 5/1A-16.6(a))  
 
These designated agencies are the Secretary of State’s Driver Services and Vehicle Services 
Departments, the Department of Human Services, the Department of Healthcare and Family 
Services, the Department of Employment Security, and the Department on Aging; however, those 
agencies which have implemented Automatic Voter Registration (“AVR”) under Section 1A-16.7 of 
the Election Code are no longer designated agencies for this purpose. (10 ILCS 5/1A-16.6) 
 
Within 2 days of receiving this type of application, if the information provided by the applicant 
matches the information on the Secretary of State’s database, the SBE shall forward to the county
